=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an undirected tree T with N vertices, numbered 1, 2, \ldots, N. The i-th edge is an undirected edge connecting vertices A_i and B_i.
A graph is defined to be an alkane if and only if it satisfies the following conditions:

- The graph is an undirected tree.
- Every vertex has degree 1 or 4, and there is at least one vertex of degree 4.

Determine whether there exists a subgraph of T that is an alkane, and if so, find the maximum number of vertices in such a subgraph.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 B_1
A_2 B_2
\vdots
A_{N - 1} B_{N - 1}

Output

If there exists a subgraph of T that is an alkane, print the maximum number of vertices in such a subgraph. Otherwise, print -1.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i \leq N
- The given graph is an undirected tree.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

9
1 2
2 3
3 4
4 5
2 6
2 7
3 8
3 9

Sample Output 1

8

Let (u, v) denote an undirected edge between vertices u and v.
A subgraph consisting of vertices 1,2,3,4,6,7,8,9 and edges (1,2),(2,3),(3,4),(2,6),(2,7),(3,8),(3,9) is an alkane.

Sample Input 2

7
1 2
1 3
2 4
2 5
3 6
3 7

Sample Output 2

-1

Sample Input 3

15
8 5
2 9
1 12
6 11
9 3
15 1
7 12
7 13
10 5
6 9
5 1
1 9
4 5
6 14

Sample Output 3

11

- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Now, but H is a forest. We need compute g for all nodes in postorder. Since g[v] depends on children g. f[v] depends on children g. We can compute in one DFS.

Implementation plan:
- Read N, edges.
- Build adjacency list for original T.
- deg = [len(adj[i])].
- good = deg[i] >= 4.
- Build H adjacency: for each edge (a,b), if good[a] and good[b]: add to h_adj[a].append(b), h_adj[b].append(a).
- If no good vertices: print(-1) return.
- Need root each component of H. Since H is forest. We can do iterative stack to get parent and order:
  visited = [False]*(N+1)
  f = [0]*(N+1); g = [0]*(N+1)
  ans = 0
  For each start in 1..N:
    if good[start] and not visited[start]:
      # traverse component
      parent = {start:0} maybe arrays parent.
      order = []
      stack = [start]; visited[start]=True; parent[start]=0
      while stack:
        v=stack.pop(); order.append(v)
        for to in h_adj[v]:
          if to == parent[v]: continue
          if not visited[to]:
             visited[to]=True; parent[to]=v; stack.append(to)
      # process reversed(order)
      for v in reversed(order):
         child_g = []
         for to in h_adj[v]:
            if parent[to] == v: # child
               child_g.append(g[to])
         # Need top 4 and top 3 sums
         child_g.sort(reverse=True)
         sum4 = sum(child_g[:4])
         sum3 = sum(child_g[:3])
         f[v] = 1 + sum4
         g[v] = 1 + sum3
         ans = max(ans, f[v])
  print(ans if ans>0 else -1)

Implementation details:
```
import sys
def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N=int(input())
    adj=[[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    edges=[]
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b=map(int,input().split())
        adj[a].append(b); adj[b].append(a)
        edges.append((a,b))
    deg=[len(adj[i]) for i in range(N+1)]
    good=[False]*(N+1)
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        if deg[i]>=4:
            good[i]=True
    h=[[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for a,b in edges:
        if good[a] and good[b]:
            h[a].append(b); h[b].append(a)
    if not any(good[1:]):
        print(-1); return
    visited=[False]*(N+1)
    parent=[0]*(N+1)
    f=[0]*(N+1)
    g=[0]*(N+1)
    ans=0
    for start in range(1,N+1):
        if good[start] and not visited[start]:
            order=[]
            stack=[start]
            visited[start]=True
            parent[start]=0
            while stack:
                v=stack.pop()
                order.append(v)
                for to in h[v]:
                    if to == parent[v]:
                        continue
                    if not visited[to]:
                        visited[to]=True
                        parent[to]=v
                        stack.append(to)
            for v in reversed(order):
                # collect g of children
                # We can avoid sorting by top4 manual for efficiency.
                top = []
                for to in h[v]:
                    if parent[to] == v:
                        val = g[to]
                        # insert into top up to 4
                # But h[v] includes parent; parent[parent?] not v. Need check parent[to]==v.
                # Use top4 list.
```
Need be careful in traversal: Since H is forest, using visited. In stack DFS, when at v, for each neighbor to not visited, set parent[to]=v and push. Since H is tree, no cycles except parent; but because undirected, when at v, a neighbor to could already be visited and not parent? In a tree, no cross edges, so only parent. But with iterative stack, if using visited set before push, no issue. Could skip `if to == parent[v]` but if neighbor already visited and not parent impossible. We can just:
```
for to in h[v]:
    if not visited[to]:
        visited[to]=True; parent[to]=v; stack.append(to)
```
This works for tree. Need ensure parent of root 0; h no 0.

Then process reversed(order). For each v, compute top4 and top3 of g[child]. Since h[v] may include parent; parent[to]==v identifies children. For root, no parent. We can compute:
```
vals=[]
for to in h[v]:
    if parent[to] == v:
        vals.append(g[to])
vals.sort(reverse=True)
f[v] = 1 + sum(vals[:4])
g[v] = 1 + sum(vals[:3])
ans = max(ans, f[v])
```

Sum of d log d <= (sum d) log max d = O(N log N). For 2e5, okay. But Python sorting many small lists okay. Could implement top4 to be safe O(N). Let's consider manual top4 to avoid any risk of TLE? Sorting 2e5 ints is trivial. But if one list 2e5, sort okay. Total O(N log N) acceptable. However, if all vertices in a path, each list small, sort overhead okay. We'll use manual top4 maybe elegant and O(N). But sorting simpler. Need final code robust.

```

        top.sort(reverse=True)
sum4 = sum(top)
sum3 = sum(top[:3])
```
Since each vertex processes its children; total operations O(sum deg * 4 log4) if sorting small. Fine. But if using `top.sort(reverse=True)` for each insertion, at most 4 elements, constant. Or use `heapq`? Not needed.

Actually for g[v], need top3; for f[v], top4. We can maintain top4; sum3 = sum(top4[:3]) if len>=3 else sum(top4). Since all positive. Good.

Implementation of top4:
```
top = []
for to in h[v]:
    if parent[to] == v:
        val = g[to]
        if len(top) < 4:
            top.append(val)
            top.sort(reverse=True)
        elif val > top[-1]:
            top[-1] = val
            top.sort(reverse=True)
f[v] = 1 + sum(top)
g[v] = 1 + sum(top[:3])
```

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the DP if a vertex v has original degree exactly4 and H degree4, but d_C(v)=3 and uses parent as leaf. Then parent is a good vertex not in C. But parent's original degree in S is1. However, parent might be adjacent to v and also to its own parent
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    N = int(input())
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]
    edges = []

    for _ in range(N - 1):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        adj[a].append(b)
        adj[b].append(a)
        edges.append((a, b))

    deg = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        deg[i] = len(adj[i])

    good = [False] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        if deg[i] >= 4:
            good[i] = True

    if not any(good):
        print(-1)
        return

    # H is the induced forest of vertices whose original degree is at least 4.
    h = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]
    for a, b in edges:
        if good[a] and good[b]:
            h[a].append(b)
            h[b].append(a)

    visited = [False] * (N + 1)
    parent = [0] * (N + 1)

    # f[v]: max branching vertices in a valid connected subset containing v,
    #       when v has no selected parent.
    # g[v]: same, but v already has a selected parent, so v can use at most 3 children.
    f = [0] * (N + 1)
    g = [0] * (N + 1)

    ans = 0

    for start in range(1, N + 1):
        if not good[start] or visited[start]:
            continue

        order = []
        stack = [start]
        visited[start] = True
        parent[start] = 0

        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            order.append(v)
            for to in h[v]:
                if not visited[to]:
                    visited[to] = True
                    parent[to] = v
                    stack.append(to)

        for v in reversed(order):
            top = []

            for to in h[v]:
                if parent[to] == v:
                    val = g[to]
                    if len(top) < 4:
                        top.append(val)
                        top.sort(reverse=True)
                    elif val > top[-1]:
                        top[-1] = val
                        top.sort(reverse=True)

            f[v] = 1 + sum(top)
            g[v] = 1 + sum(top[:3])

            if f[v] > ans:
                ans = f[v]

    print(3 * ans + 2)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
